2026. 11.2
先週（10/26～10/30）発表の主な経済指標
AI関連を中心に設備投資が増勢を維持し、産業用機械にも広
がり。好調な企業収益も投資を後押し。個人消費も堅調。金
融所得や移転所得、株高による資産効果が購買力を下支え
政策要因を除く都区部CPIは上昇基調。財価格の上昇が目立
つ一方、サービス価格改定の広がりは限定的。企業の価格転嫁
は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが値上げの重荷に
注目点② 日本で進む価格転嫁、節約志向との綱引きが続く注目点①米国経済は投資の裾野拡大と消費の強さを確認
7～9月期GDPは成⾧加速。AI関連を中心とする設
備投資の拡大に加え、個人消費も堅調推移。9月
のコアPCE物価は3％を超える伸びが継続。景気の
底堅さを背景にFRBにもう一段の金融引き締め余地

労働分配率は低下傾向。賃金
以外の所得が家計所得を補完
米国：企業収益が拡大する一方、労働分配率は低下。金融所得や移転所得が消費を下支え
 企業収益が好調を維持する一方、労働所得の対GDP比は低下傾向。金融所得や政府からの移転所得が家計所得を補完
― 株式配当や社会保障給付など、賃金以外の所得が家計の購買力を支える構図
 こうした所得環境に加え、株高による資産効果も消費を下支え。10月入り後も個人消費は底堅く推移
― 燃料高にもかかわらず、空港利用者数は前年並みの水準を維持し、小売売上高も高めの伸びを維持
― 年末商戦も前年を上回る伸びが予想されている

日本：コアCPIは10月に鈍化も、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調
4
 10月の都区部コアＣＰＩは前年比＋2.6％（前月：同＋2.7％）と伸び鈍化
― 無償化期間の時期ズレによる9月水道料金の一時的な押し上げが剥落したことに加え、コメ価格の下落が影響
 ただし、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調。サービス物価が底堅く推移する中、財物価の加速がインフレ率押し上げ
― 原油高等によるコスト増の転嫁が進展し、エネルギー、その他財（除く水道料金）の価格上昇が足元で加速
― 春先に鈍化した一般サービス物価（除く家賃）も人件費等の転嫁進展を映じて前年比＋2％近傍で推移

―10月は外食を中心に価格改定の動きがみられるも、広がりは限定的。一方、外食除く改定品目では値上げ幅が拡大
（注） 食品は、コメと外食を除く食料価格。水道料金はその他財に該当。サービスは一般
サービスと公共サービスの合計（家賃を除く）

日本：企業の価格転嫁は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが追加値上げの重荷に
5
 企業の価格転嫁は着実に進展
― 日商LOBO調査では、コスト増加分の4割以上を価格転嫁できた企業が55.4％と4月から2.6％ポイント増加。ただし、労
務費の転嫁は40.6％にとどまり、販売単価DIも小幅低下するなど、中小企業では追加値上げの勢いに一服感
 消費者態度指数は2カ月連続で悪化。消費マインドの弱さは残存
― 物価高による節約志向が企業の追加値上げを制約している可能性。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が2％に到達したと判
断し、上振れリスクへの警戒を強化。ただし、サービス価格の改定に広がりはみられず
（出所） 日本商工会議所「LOBO調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（出所） 内閣府「消費動向調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 7

米国：10月FOMC～据え置き、ウォーシュ議長は引き締めがAIセクターにも影響との認識示す
6
 3.75～4.00％に据え置き（2人が反対、利上げを支持）。景気評価を上方修正しつつ、9月利上げ効果を見極め
― 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ。ウォーシュ議長は投資の広がりに言及
 AIセクターでは投資の選別が起きており、高い成長期待を持つ同セクターに対しても金融政策は有効との認識示す
 2027・2028年の会合の一部を政策決定を行わず、声明文も公表しない戦略的議論の会合とすると表明
AIセクター、生産性、タスクフォース声明文の変更、政策判断、会合運営
 反対2票は利上げを主張
 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ
 様子見による「情報のさらなる蓄積」
が、物価安定への「適時の復帰」のため
の政策判断に役立つ
声明文
 「以前より引き締まった」が「引き締め
的な水準にあるわけではない」と述べ、
追加利上げの余地残す
 「参加者の多数派は利上げ効果が出てい
ると認識」と言及

金融環境
と効果
 「政治は、この決定に何の役割も果たし
ていない」と言明 中間選挙
 2027年秋の1回、2028年には2回の会合
を政策決定せず議論だけの会合へ
 戦略的な熟議のための時間と余地確保
会合運営
 「投資家が期待する収益率はあまりに違
うので、AIには何の影響も及ばない」と
いう見方を否定
 「新技術と今回の投資急増」に対して金
融政策は有効
 信用スプレッドの拡大などは「市場参加
者が、どのような投資を行いたいか、資
本配分を選別している」ことの表れ
AI投資
 「経済全体の生産性は、ここ2、3年ほど
上向いて」おり、高い経済成⾧率によっ
てインフレ圧力が生まれ、利上げが必要
な状況ではないとの認識
生産性
11月16日に生産性・雇用の公聴会。タスク
フォース報告は年末が目安（一部は遅延の
可能性）。バランスシート提言は2027年1
～3月期の早期を想定
タスク
フォース

### Page 8

内容ポイント
 26年3Qの景気は引き続き拡大
 エネルギー価格と資金調達コストの上昇が今後の重しに景気
 エネルギー価格の見通しは9月の予想を上回っている
 今後数四半期の物価は目標を大幅に上回る見通し。その後
はエネルギー価格低下と金利上昇により目標水準に回帰へ
 物価の基調・賃金はおおむね安定。ただし、価格形成の初
期段階では上昇圧力がみられる
 今後6週間で得られる、基調的なインフレに関する多くの情
報を、とりわけ注視していく
物価
 9月理事会以降、金融環境は引き締まっている。金利は上昇
し、株価は下落している
 期待インフレの上昇やハイパースケーラーの社債発行等、
複合的な要因が金利を押し上げ。加えて、債務の多い加盟
国は固有の課題に直面
 市場の不当かつ無秩序な動向が金融政策伝達の妨げとな
る場合、対応手段は用意されている
金融
環境
 全会一致で金利据え置きも、利上げが適切かどうか議論し
た。「今利上げを実施してはどうか」との意見もあった
 今後の金融政策はデータに基づき、会合ごとに決定

政策

金利水準 運転資金・在庫

（「増加」－「減少」、％）
欧州：ECBは政策金利を据え置き。高金利は企業の資金需要を押し下げ
7
 ECBは預金ファシリティ金利を2.5％で据え置き。利上げが議論されるも物価の基調に著変なく、慎重な対応が適切と判断
― 利上げの要否検討にあたり、エネルギー価格の上昇と金融環境の引き締まりが主な論点に。今後は物価の基調に関す
る指標を特に注視する姿勢。ラガルド総裁は、進むべき方向が「きわめて明確」であると発言
― ラガルド総裁は2027年2月に早期退任へ。同年5月までに専務理事2名を含む計3名の幹部が交代する見込み
 10月の銀行貸出調査によれば、金利水準が企業の資金需要減少に寄与。高金利の影響は既に一部で顕在化
― 同調査によれば、企業向け貸出基準はフランスで一段と厳格化。銀行のリスク許容度低下やリスク認識の高まりが主因

企業向け銀行貸出の需要増減要因
（注） 需要増減は、企業向け貸出の需要が前四半期から「かなり増加」「やや増加」と回
答した銀行の割合から、「かなり減少」「やや減少」と回答した銀行の割合を引いた
もの。「その他要因」には他の調達手段による代替や、「運転資金・在庫」「設備投
資」以外の資金需要発生等を含む。2026年Q3の調査期間は9月21日～10月6日
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
10月ECB理事会のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
金利水準の高さが
貸出需要を下押し

### Page 9

英国：政府は財政規律を重視しつつエネルギー高対策に注力。財政余力は縮小
 英政府は秋季財政計画を公表。財政規律を重視しつつ家計や小規模事業者向け支援等を打ち出す内容
― 燃料税減税と電気料金へのVAT減税の延長、給付金支給によるエネルギー高対策、住宅購入支援などの家計支援策
を発表。小規模事業者の税負担軽減や電気料金の引き下げなど、企業向け支援も公表
― 上記政策の財源は銀行への追加課税やギャンブルへの課税強化等で確保する方針
― 防衛費は2030年までにGDP比3％へ引き上げる方針。ただし、財源を含む詳細は来年の歳出見直しに先送り
 財政余力は前回3月の237億ポンドから147億ポンドに大幅縮小。中東情勢悪化に伴う金利上昇等が背景
英国：財政余力（左）と3月以降の財政余力減少要因（右）
（出所）OBRより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
英国：秋季財政計画の主な内容
（出所） 英国政府より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
• 燃料税5ペンス減税や電気料金のVA T免除を来年7月まで実施
• 2,000 万世帯を対象に100ポンドのエネルギー高支援給付金支給

• 使用量にかかわらず課される家庭用ガスのスタンディングチャー
ジ廃止
• エネルギー集約型製造業1万社の電気代を20～25％軽減
• 手ごろな住宅建設に15億ポンド追加拠出。住宅初回購入者向け
支援「Your First Home」に47億ポンドを充当
• 小規模事業者の事業用不動産税免除対象を拡大。パブや音楽会
場等には同税を追加で20％軽減
• 食品等100件以上の品目の関税撤廃
• 防衛投資計画で必要とされていた150億ポンドの追加投資全額を
拠出。2030年に防衛費GDP比3％達成を企図。来年の歳出見直
しで財源の詳細公表
• 地方分権の一環として、来年から事業用不動産税のうち20％を地
方に移転、2030年までに100％への引き上げを目指すほか、所得
税も2028年から一部を地方に配分しインフラ整備の財源に
• 銀行への上乗せ課税を3％から6％に引き上げ
• 高額賭博機へのギャンブル税を20％から25％に引き上げ
• 貯蓄・不動産所得の税率を2％Pt引き上げ

英国：財政懸念を背景とする金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
 財政計画がおおむね事前の予想どおりの内容であったことなどから英10年債利回りの動きは限定的。ただし、足元の高金
利の要因であるインフレや財政懸念、世界的なAI関連投資増加は今後も続くとみられ、金利は高止まりが予想される

 利払い費による財政圧迫継続が予想される中、財政懸念を背景とする一段の金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
― 高金利継続による利払い負担が財政を圧迫。財政を巡る不確実性が高い状況は続く見通し
― 欧州各国では財政懸念や政情不安を背景に高債務国中心に国債利回り上昇
― 財政への懸念を払拭しにくい状況が続くなか、英国でも金利急騰とこれに伴う財政悪化リスクには要注意
政府債務残高と10年債利回り変化幅の関係

中国：製造業利益は引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引。7～9月期の利益率は前期を下回る
 9月の製造業総利益は前年比＋14.4％と前月（同＋9.1％）から加速。引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引
― コンピューター・通信機器は同＋72.9％の増益。石油・石炭加工は単月赤字から一転し、同10倍超の伸び
― 他方、鉄鋼は単月赤字に転落したほか、自動車も同▲68.5％の減益。過剰生産業種は厳しい状況
 7～9月期の製造業の売上高利益率は5.0％と4～6月期（5.5％）から低下。自動車など幅広い業種で前期を下回る
― コンピューター・通信機器は7.4％で、前年（4.3％）から急速に改善。資源高で非鉄金属加工も好調

― 他方、自動車は2.5％と、前年（3.8％）から大幅に悪化。過当競争による販売価格の低下が響いた格好

 中東情勢緊迫化後、食料や燃料を中心にインフレ率が急上昇したタイとフィリピンでは、4～6月期に消費の伸びが減速

 背景に実質賃金の鈍化による購買力の低下
― 購買力低下を受け、タイは6月～11月に消費支援策（食品・日用品などの購入補助）を実施、フィリピンは政府による企
業への補助も伴いながら首都圏の最低賃金の大幅な引き上げを6月に発令。物価上昇への対応が両国の政治課題に

日本：基調的物価は2％到達も安定化には距離。日銀は利上げの影響を見極め微妙な舵取りへ
13
日銀10月会合のポイント（展望レポート・総裁会見）
（出所）日本銀行「経済・物価情勢の展望」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
 日銀は基調的物価について「2％目標と概ね整合的な水準」に評価変更も、10月会合では利上げを見送り
― 今後は物価を2％で安定化させる局面へ。金融環境は緩和的との評価を維持し、次回12月会合で追加利上げの公算大
― 経済・物価はオントラックとの評価。原油価格の想定上振れを一部反映も見通し修正は軽微
― 総裁会見では「直近で物価上振れリスクが高まったわけではない」との発言。一段の利上げ加速は喫緊の必要性なし
 利上げを通じた基調的物価への影響を見極める手がかりとして、日銀は金融環境を注視

― 銀行・企業向けアンケート調査や銀行貸出残高にみられる変化、グローバル金融市場からの影響波及には要注意
経済・物価見通し
（注） 青字＝上昇修正、赤字＝下方修正。ドバイ原油価格は1バレル105ドル程度を出発
点に70ドル程度まで下落していくと前提。飲食料品消費税率の引き下げの影響は
織り込まず
（出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
全体として修正は軽微。日銀見通し
対比でオントラックとの評価
展望レポート
• 基調的物価上昇率は2%物価目標と概ね整合的な水準
• 実質金利は上昇しているが、短期中心になお低水準。資金需要、
資産市場動向等も踏まえると金融環境は依然緩和した状態
• 名目賃金は前年並みのベースアップが実現するもとで、着実な増
加が続く見込み
• 海外の多くの中央銀行が利上げ方向に政策を転換。グローバルな
金融環境の変化がわが国の経済・物価に及ぼす影響に留意
会見中の総裁発言
• 物価上振れリスクはなおも無視できないが、1カ月前から物価上振
れリスクは大きく高まったわけではない

• 基調的物価が2%にアンカーされている度合いは極めて低い
• 金融政策が適時適切に発動され、緩和度合いを調整していくこと
で期待が安定化されていく
• 本支店ヒアリングでは、中小零細を中心に資金繰りがタイトになり
つつあるという声が聞かれ、今後の広がりを注意深くみていく
• 早めの政策対応を基本方針とするべき状況までは至っていない

日本：AI関連財の増産を背景に設備投資は今後拡大も、輸入依存の高さが成長の抑制要因に
14
 9月鉱工業生産は前月比＋2.2％と3カ月ぶりに増産。26年に入ってから増産基調を維持
― 増産の主因はAI関連。旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置などの増産が鉱工業生産をけん引
― 加えて、自動車生産の持ち直しなど、熊本地震や豪雨による下振れからの回復も生産の押し上げに寄与
 AI関連を中心に増産基調の生産動向は、今後の設備投資拡大を示唆
― ただし、資本財の輸入依存度は上昇傾向。AI関連業種で設備不足感が強まるなか、資本財の供給を輸入に頼る構造
― 人手不足などの供給制約で国内メーカーの増産が追いつかず、拡大する投資需要の一部が海外に流出の可能性も

2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し
～転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方を探る～
（2026年10月2日発刊）
調査部レポートのご紹介 PR
2026年10月作成
レポート
URL
レポート
二次元
コード
世界経済はどこへ向かうのか？中東危機やAI・レジリエンス関連の投資が
もたらす変化や、経済・金融市場の行方を展望
【ポイント①】
中東危機が⾧期化するなかでも、AI・レジリエンス関連の旺盛な投資需要に
支えられ、世界経済は3％台の成⾧ペースを維持
【ポイント②】
世界はインフレ・高金利の新常態へ。成⾧・負担の偏在が経済主体間の
「耐性格差」を拡大
【ポイント③】
海外主要中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ、日銀も物価上振れを警戒

### Page 1

Lite-On Tech

Upgrade to Buy; 3Q26 beat, better margin; 
solid AI outlook; lift PO to NT$288

Rating Change: BUY | PO: 288.00 TWD | Price: 240.50 TWD

Upgrade to Buy and raise PO to NT$288  
We upgrade Lite-On Tech (LoT) to Buy from Neutral with a higher PO of NT$288 (18x 
2027E EPS; prior NT$285 with valuation unchanged). We turn more positive on LoT 
after two straight quarters of results beat, especially with margin surprise. We lift 2026-
28E EPS by 1-8% (Exhibit 1), with still solid AI power-supply demand/order. Our 2026-
28E EPS is 4-7% above consensus (Exhibit 2). We expect the market to react more 
positively to LoT, with 3Q26 results beat, vs. Delta with 3Q26 results miss (see Robert 
Cheng’s Delta 3Q26 note), amid similar positive comments on long-term AI market 
demand. 
3Q26 beat: better margins, despite lighter sales 
LoT’s 3Q26 GM/OPM of 25.9%/15.1% came in better than BofAe of 25.3%/13.5% and 
consensus of 25.1%/13.6%, despite lighter sales (3%/5% below BofAe/consensus). We 
view the QoQ GM/OPM decline from record high in 2Q26 as largely expected as mgmt. 
had indicated at 2Q26 meeting that 1H26 GM of 24.7% represented a more sustainable 
level. Besides AI, better margins were also helped by improving product mix in IT/CE (i.e. 
higher mix from high-end ICT and LEO satellite) and higher automation level.  
4Q26 sales up QoQ; 2H26 GM above 1H; rising AI mix 
Similar to Delta, LoT also sees short-term shipment uncertainties/push-outs due to 
component shortage and power/electricity shortage for AI datacenter infrastructure. 
Nevertheless, the CEO remains long-term positive on AI demand. He also expects easing 
component/power constraints from 4Q26. Yet, mgmt. expects 4Q26 cloud sales to grow 
QoQ/YoY, led by ASIC/GPU/AMD projects, and 2H26 GM to be above 1H26 level (BofAe: 
26.3% in 4Q26, vs. 25.9%/24.7% in 3Q26/1H26). The CEO indicated 800VDC power rack 
mass shipment in 1Q27, after sample shipment in 4Q26. Mgmt. expects AI sales mix at 
30%+ in 2026 and 40-50% in 2027. This time, LoT raised 2026 capex to NT$19.7bn 
(prior NT$18.0bn) and sees higher level in 2027 (BofAe: NT$20.0bn).

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 9.

Company Sector 
Electronics Manufacturing Services

Company Description 
Lite-On Tech is a leading PC and server power-supply maker 
globally. Besides PC/server power-supply, its key products 
include optoelectronics (photo-coupler, LED lighting, 
outdoor lighting, auto lighting) and power-supply for game 
console and industrial. Key applications include AIoT, 5G, 
and smart life. Its key customers include HP, Dell, Lenovo, 
Microsoft, Sony, Nintendo, and global cloud hyperscalers. 
Lite-On Tech was founded in 1975 and headquartered in 
Taiwan.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Lite-On Tech in view of (1) its solid 
position in AI server power-supply as the second largest 
component maker globally and (2) expanding margins with 
improving product mix, rising scale, and better operating 
efficiency. We see consistently high cash dividend payout 
(over 50% since 2008) and solid balance sheet as share 
price downside supports.

Stock Data  
Price to Book Value 5.3x

### Page 3

Upgrade to Buy; raise PO to NT$288  
We upgrade Lite-On Tech (LoT) to Buy, from Neutral, with a higher PO of NT$288 (18x 
2027E EPS) from NT$285 with valuation unchanged. We turn more positive on LoT after 
two straight quarters of results beat, especially with margin surprise. We lift 2026-28E 
EPS by 1-8% (Exhibit 1) to reflect 3Q26 results beat, the latest guidance, and solid AI 
power-supply demand/order. Our 2026-28E EPS is 4-7% above consensus (Exhibit 2).  
LoT’s share price corrected 21% and 14% in the recent three months and one month, 
respectively, vs. key peer Delta’s (2308 TT; covered by Robert Cheng) -27%/-29% and 
TAIEX’s -2%/-6%. We expect more positive market reaction to LoT’s 3Q26 results beat, 
vs. Delta’s 3Q26 results miss (see Robert Cheng’s Delta 3Q26 note), amid similar 
positive comments on long-term AI market demand. LoT also has a cheaper valuation vs. 
Delta. LoT currently trades at 15x 2027E P/E, below Delta’s 24x and overall peers’ 
average of 20x (Exhibit 6).

3Q26 beat: better margins, despite lighter sales 
LoT posted 3Q26 results and held the physical analyst meeting on 30 Oct after market 
close. 3Q26 GM/OPM of 25.9%/15.1% came in better than BofAe of 25.3%/13.5% and 
consensus of 25.1%/13.6%, despite lighter sales (3%/5% below BofAe/consensus; +9% 
QoQ, +28% YoY). We view the QoQ decline in GM/OPM from the record highs in 2Q26

### Page 4

as largely expected, as management had already indicated at the 2Q26 meeting that the 
1H26 GM of 24.7% represented a more sustainable level (see our 2Q26 result note). 
Besides AI, we believe better margins were also helped by improving product mix in 
IT/CE (i.e. higher mix from high-end ICT and LEO satellite) and higher automation level. 
With non-operating gain of NT$1.1bn (including net interest income of NT$369mn and 
FX gain of NT$734mn) and higher minority interest, net income (+4% QoQ, +60% YoY) 
was 12%/11% above BofAe/consensus.

Similar comments on material/power shortage like Delta 
Similar to Delta mgmt.’s comments at 3Q26 analyst meeting, LoT also sees short-term 
shipment uncertainties/push-outs due to material/component shortage (e.g. semis, PCB, 
passives) and power/electricity shortage for AI datacenter infrastructure. Nevertheless, 
LoT’s CEO remains long-term positive on AI demand with ongoing platform/spec 
upgrade. He also expects easing component/power constraints from 4Q26, with ongoing 
strategic component procurement/preparation and customers’ robust AI demand.

4Q26 sales up QoQ; 2H26 GM above 1H; rising AI mix 
Into 4Q26, mgmt. expects cloud sales to grow both QoQ and YoY. Key drivers include (1) 
volume production of 8.5kW PSUs for ASIC applications and BBUs, next-generation 
Nvidia Vera Rubin 110kW solutions, 42-inch power shelves for AMD Helios, (2) prototype 
shipments of 800VDC power rack, and (3) continued momentum in high-end power 
applications, with shipments of power solutions for LEO satellites. the CEO also expects 
2H26 GM to be above 1H26 level. We thus project 4Q26 overall sales to grow 5% QoQ 
(+36% YoY), with GM at 26.3% (vs. 25.9%/24.7% in 3Q26/1H26). For HVDC, the CEO 
further stated 800VDC power rack mass shipment in 1Q27, after final validation and 
sample shipment in 4Q26. Mgmt. kept AI sales mix target at 30%+ in 2026 and 
potentially 40-50% in 2027. LoT raised 2026 capex to NT$19.7bn (vs. NT$18.0bn guided 
at 2Q26 analyst meeting) and expects a higher level in 2027 (BofAe: NT$20.0bn).

Initially expects 2027 sales/GM/OPM YoY increase 
Into 2027, the CEO initially expects cloud sales to grow another 70% YoY, driven by 
rising ASP and power-supply product/spec upgrade. He targets another 10% YoY sales 
growth for optoelectronics business, driven by industrial and new energy business. He 
expects LEO satellite power to grow YoY but sees flattish sales from conventional ICT 
power business. Nevertheless, he expects GM/OPM YoY expansion in 2027. We thus 
expect 2027 sales to grow 38% YoY, with GM/OPM increasing by 1.6ppt/1.5ppt YoY.

### Page 7

Price objective basis & risk 
Delta Electronics Inc. (DLTEF) 
Our PO of NT$2,250 is based on 30x 4Q27-3Q28E EPS, which is close to 2.0 standard 
deviations above Delta's average trading P/E since 2012. We believe the P/E multiple is 
justified, given (1) continuous strength and leading position in server power-supply and 
power choke business, (2) content/value growth with broader product offering in AI 
(artificial intelligence) business, (3) rising cooling sales by targeting top CSPs' 
customized projects, and (4) ongoing margin expansion.

The upside risks to our PO are (1) stronger-than-expected server/datacenter demand, (2) 
faster-than-expected IA recovery, and (3) faster-than-expected EV model launches by 
global brands.

The downside risks to our PO are (1) unexpected changes in policy that could impact IA 
growth in China, (2) slower-than-expected server/datacenter demand recovery, (3) 
earnings impact from unprofitable long-term investments (4) Adverse effects of 
announced USITC 337 investigation into multiple companies in the AI supply chain, 
which includes Delta Electric. 
Lite-On Tech (LOTZF) 
Our PO of NT$288 is based on 18x 2027E EPS, which is around 1.75SD above its 
historical average since 2013. We believe the P/E multiple is justified, given its solid 
market position to capture multi-year AI server power-supply market demand with 
improving product mix. Yet, we also assign a valuation discount to its key peer, given its 
late entry and a slower progress in cooling business, smaller scale, and lighter margin 
profile.

Downside risks: (1) weaker-than-expected demand from server/cloud power-supply 
business, (2) lighter margin profile for automotive LED lighting with limited ASP 
expansion, (3) pricing competition and component price increase from power-supply 
business, and (4) less order win from cooling business.

Upside risks: (1) stronger-than-expected margin profile for automotive LED lighting with 
ASP expansion, (2) faster revenue growth from server/cloud power-supply with a more 
positive datacenter market outlook, and (3) larger/stronger order win from cooling 
business.

Industry View In-Line
(-) PC OEMs and ODMs: AMD (covered by Joseph Moore) mentioned that PC sell-
through slowed down in late 3Q, particularly in the consumer segment, and that it
estimates PC units will fall in double digits (%) in CY27. This aligns with Morgan

Stanley's estimate at a ~11% y/y decline (report link). AMD also mentioned that it
expects to outgrow the market in CY27.
(+) Server OEMs, ODMs, and component makers: AMD expects CPU TAM to reach
US$220bn by 2030, implying a ~50% CAGR. It also mentioned that its Venice CPU
began shipments in 3Q and cloud providers are deploying it in 4Q.
(+) ABF substrates:  AMD said that it has secured the wafers, substrates, advanced
packaging, and memory needed for much higher 2027 growth, and is planning to add

more. It also mentioned that its supply chain is quite "fungible" across its PC and
server business, but overall it still "has more demand than it has supply."

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Idea
3Q preview: Co-Tech is scheduled to report its 3Q results on November 6. We
estimate 3Q revenue at NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y), primarily driven by
higher copper prices and product-mix shifts. We lower our 3Q GM estimate from
28.2% to 27.0% (-30bps q/q, +650bps y/y), reflecting the impact of higher
production costs and copper prices during the quarter. Co-Tech has already reported
preliminary monthly profits for August and September at the request of the TWSE

(report link); combined August and September net profit now accounts for
approximately 67% of our 3Q estimate. We estimate 3Q net profit at NT$610mn
(+12% q/q, +141% y/y), implying EPS of NT$2.41.
4Q GM should improve sequentially: We expect 4Q revenue to decline slightly q/q
to NT$3,074mn (-3% q/q, +42% y/y), primarily due to the end of production for low-
end HTE copper foil and the lack of new capacity coming online. However, we expect
Co-Tech’s GM to expand 700bps q/q to 34%, driven by an improved product mix, a

~10% processing-fee increase effective from 4Q onward, and lower electricity rates
compared with 2Q–3Q, when summer electricity rates were in effect. We forecast
4Q EPS of NT$3.02 (+25% q/q, +102% y/y). For CY26, we estimate revenue will
increase approximately 47% y/y to NT$11.6bn, with GM expanding 760bps y/y to
29.3%, implying EPS of NT$9.62 (+129% y/y).
Potential processing-fee hike: Our supply-chain checks suggest that a key
competitor may announce processing-fee increases as early as December. If so, we

expect Co-Tech to follow with a price increase as well.
Upward revisions to our capacity assumptions ( Exhibit 3 ): Co-Tech has
announced plans to expand its greenfield capacity in Malaysia (report link). The
Malaysian expansion will be implemented in two phases, adding approximately 20k
tons of annual HVLP3/4 capacity from late 2028 onward. RG-series capacity is also
expected to increase modestly through the conversion of older HTE production
lines.

Raised PT to NT$745; stay OW: The increase in our price target is driven by our
earnings estimate changes for CY26-28. Our new price target implies 21x CY28 P/E
and the stock currently trades at only 12x CY28 P/E. We also find the valuation
attractive compared to  its past 3Y P/E average of ~20x.

Price target and valuation methodology
Our price target for Co-Tech increases  ~2% to NT$745: This implies 39x CY27 or 21x
CY28 P/E. Our price target is also our base case scenario value. It is derived from our

residual income (RI) model (similar to the rest of our tech hardware universe).
To calculate the fair value of the stock, we use the company's beginning equity plus the
present value of all expected residual income of earnings in excess of cost of capital. The
RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and negative when it is below the
cost of capital. Our key assumptions include:
•  A cost of equity of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7%, and risk-free rate

of 1.0%),
• a medium-term growth rate of 11.5% (lowered from 13% previously, as we enter a
higher base in CY27),
• a terminal growth rate of 3%.
The 21x CY28 P/E implied by our price target is above its  past 3Y average  of 20x. We deem
this valuation as fair, because Co-Tech is benefitting from an earnings growth cycle fueled
by stronger AI server/networking CCL demand, Co-Tech's continued mix improvement
(from HTE + RTF (RG) copper foils to advanced RTF (RG) + HVLP3/4 copper foils) and

continued  processing fee adjustments, which supports margin expansion. The stock is
trading at just 12x CY28 P/E, below Co-Tech's past 3Y average of 20x. Thus, we view the
risk reward as attractive.
Our bull- and bear-case values also increase ~2% to NT$1,225 and NT$305,
respectively.

MS Rating
▪ Co-Tech has exited the low-margin HTE
copper foil business, which should support
proﬁtability improvement.
▪ Co-Tech has a dominant share in the
server R-PCB copper foil market with its RG
copper foils (equivalent to HVLP1/2), making
it a key beneﬁciary of global data center
infrastructure build-outs.
▪ Co-Tech is proactively expanding its
HVLP4 capacity to support key AI
customers' high-end copper foil demand. It
should have a 22% supply share by 2028, on

our estimates.
▪ Risk-reward is attractive with the stock
trading at 12x CY28 P/E vs the 3Y historical
average of 20x.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Summary: SDI reported its 3Q26 results after market hours on 5 November. Its 3Q26 EPS 
was TWD1.94, beating our EPS estimate (TWD1.79) but missing the Bloomberg 
consensus (TWD2.07).

We have a Buy (1) rating on SDI with a 12-month TP of TWD265, based on a PER of 35x 
on our 1-year-forward EPS. For more information on the company, please refer to our 
latest flash, 2Q26 review: net profit beat expectations, on 7 August 2026.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results beat our estimate but missed the Bloomberg consensus. SDI’s 3Q26 
revenue of TWD4,020m (+12.9% QoQ, +58.1% YoY) beat our and the consensus 
estimates by 12.4% and 2.4%, mainly due to the better-than-expected auto segment 
and rush orders for the consumer segment. Its gross margin came in at 20.5% in 3Q26 
(vs. 20.7% in 2Q26 and 12.4% in 3Q25), missing both our (20.7%) and the consensus 
(21.5%) estimates, which we attribute to a lower-than-expected yield rate and OT 
expense despite positive factors such as price hikes, a higher copper price and FX. Its 
operating profit of TWD517m (+24.0% QoQ, +399.0% YoY) beat our estimate by 13.4%, 
but was in line with the consensus estimate. Its net profit came in at TWD383m, which 
also beat our estimate (TWD339m) but missed the Bloomberg consensus (TWD400m). 
Its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.94. For 3Q26 revenue, electronics accounted for 91% 
(+14.2% QoQ, +67.3% YoY) and stationery accounted for 9% (+1.6% QoQ, +1.6% YoY). 
Within electronics, the product mix was: auto 47% (+14.2% QoQ, +63.8% YoY), 
industrial 15% (+14.2% QoQ, +19.5% YoY), consumer 28% (+6.6% QoQ, +67.3% YoY), 
AI lead frame 7% (+33.2% QoQ) and others 3% (+71.3% QoQ, +67.3% YoY).

• Bright lead frame outlook with rising AI lead frame. SDI expects 4Q26 revenue to be 
flat to slightly up QoQ and 20%+ revenue YoY growth for its 2027 lead frame business, 
due to an intact order outlook from auto, industrial and AI. It also expects its AI lead 
frame revenue is to reach 10%+ of 2027 revenue.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating on SDI with a 12-month TP of TWD265, based on a PER of 35x 
on our 1-year-forward EPS. The stock is currently trading at PERs of 25.2x/19.1x, based 
on our 2026/27E EPS, vs. its past-3-year one-year forward PER trading range of 12-72x

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

晶豪科 (3006 TT)  Trading Buy

報價收斂，後續成長動能受限，建議 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ：AI 伺服器對 HBM 多層晶圓堆疊的強勁需求大幅吸
納三大原廠先進 DRAM 產能 ， 引發傳統記憶體供給端嚴重缺口 ，
缺貨潮由 DDR5 與 DDR4 蔓延至 DDR3 、 DDR2 及 NOR/NAND 
Flash 。同業大廠如華邦電 (2344 TT) 基於晶圓廠經濟效益考量，
產能優先配置予大容量 DDR4 產品，對 DDR2 與 DDR3 採取停
供或減供措施，大幅限縮市場流通供給。目前晶豪科 DDR2 與
DDR3 合計佔 DRAM 營收比重超過 50% ，在同業減供退出的趨
勢下，晶豪科已成為市場核心主要供應商，市占地位結構性擴
張。預估晶豪科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.72 元。雖然記憶體價格
在 2027 年仍可以維持高檔，但目前報價漲幅開始收斂，產能供
給受限，成本持續上揚，後續成長動能受限，因此對晶豪科投
資建議為 Trading Buy 。  
3Q26 稅後 EPS 36.09 元： 受惠國際大廠把產能轉至 HBM、DDR5
等高階產品時，成熟型 DRAM、DDR2/DDR3 等利基型記憶體供
給減少，帶動報價上揚，晶豪科 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元，
QoQ+58.83% ，毛利率增加 8.75 個百分點至 77.44% ，3Q26 稅後
淨利 107.59 億元，QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元。1Q26~3Q26
營收 433.63 億元，年增 3.439 倍，毛利率 69.62% ，較 2025 年
同期增 60.08 個百分點，歸屬母公司淨利 187.3 億元，稅後 EPS 
64.54 元。  
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 39.41 元： 展望 4Q26 營運表現，儘管整體
出貨數量受限於上游晶圓代工與封測端產能配額難見大幅擴
增，但由於 4Q26 產品平均銷售單價 (ASP) 延續上漲趨勢，漲幅
約 10~20% 。但晶圓代工從 07/2026 漲約 45% ，封測約漲 20% ，
將會逐步反映在 4Q26 銷貨成本，經營團隊預期 4Q26 毛利率將
穩健維持在 3Q26 之歷史高檔區間，來自產品售價調升幅度足
以抵銷成本增幅。預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 39.41 元。預估 2026 年
稅後 EPS 102.25 元。

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 3 日

2 群益投顧 
  晶豪科 ESG ：  
企業永續與治理架構  
• 企業永續委員會：於 10/2020 成立，負責評估永續經營的風險與機會、制
訂永續發展願景與策略。  
• 核心推動構面：涵蓋強化公司治理、夥伴關係、環境永續、友善職場及社
會公益。  
• 永續報告書：定期依循國際標準 (如  GRI 、 TCFD 、 SASB 等 )編製與揭露
年度永續報告書 ， 可至晶豪科技 ESG 報告書或耀智永續報告書資料庫  查閱
與下載。  
環境與氣候行動  
• 減碳目標：積極因應全球氣候變遷，設定具體減碳目標，其減碳表現經評
估朝向升溫  1.5°C 以下的目標邁進 (實測減碳溫度約  1.506°C) 。  
• 永續金融連結：曾與金融機構 (如台新銀行、北富銀等 )合作推動永續績效
聯貸案 (30 億元聯貸案 )，將  ESG 指標與授信條件結合，落實綠色金融與企
業社會責任。  
社會責任與人才培育  
• 社會公益與教育：設立「晶豪科技教育基金會」，長期投入教育與社會公
益相關推廣。  
• 人才與實習計畫：積極與青年世代接軌，辦理暑期研發與  ESG 實習生計
畫，深化校園人才交流與職場體驗。  
記憶體價格調漲，晶豪科 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元， QoQ+58.83% ，稅後淨利
107.59 億元， QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元：  
晶豪科為國內利基型記憶體  IC 設計公司，產品涵蓋利基型 DRAM 產品組
合，涵蓋從傳統 SDRAM 到主流的 DDR 、 DDR2 、 DDR3 及 DDR4 。廣泛應
用於消費性電子、工業控制、網通設備、 PC 週邊，公司主要是 4Gb 以下為
主。在 Flash 方面，晶豪科有 SLC NAND Flash 以及編碼型快閃記憶體 (NOR 
Flash) 。因應中低階智慧手機、系統級封裝 (SiP) 及固態硬碟 (SSD) 需求快速
成長，公司以 MCP/eMCP/eMMC 為主力產品。類比和數位的產品
(Audio/Power/Motor Driver/Sensor) 佔公司營收比重低。各產品佔比， DRAM
佔營收比重最高，約 60~65% ， Flash 和其他約佔 30~33%(MCP 等也有含
DRAM) ，非記憶體佔營收比重 5%。  
受惠國際大廠把產能轉至 HBM 、 DDR5 等高階產品時，成熟型 DRAM 、
DDR2/DDR3 等利基型記憶體供給減少，帶動報價上揚，晶豪科 3Q26 營收
221.50 億元， QoQ+58.83% ，毛利率增加 8.75 個百分點至 77.44% ， 3Q26 稅
後淨利 107.59 億元， QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元。  
累計 1Q26~3Q26 營收 433.63 億元，年增 3.439 倍，毛利率 69.62% ，較 2025
年同期增 60.08 個百分點，歸屬母公司淨利 187.3 億元，稅後 EPS 64.54 元。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 3 日

3 群益投顧 
3Q26 底晶豪科的存貨為 90.76 億元，庫存水準較 2Q26 的 79.56 億元，
QoQ+14.08% ，要反映晶圓廠與封測調價造成的成本墊高，而非量的顯著增
加。  
合作晶圓廠現階段增產幅度相對受限，預估 4Q26 營收 243.02 億元，
QoQ+9.72% ，稅後淨利 117.49 億元， QoQ+9.20% ，稅後 EPS 39.41 元：  
晶圓代工端產能配置方面，晶豪科 DRAM 主要晶圓代工廠為力積電 (6770 
TT) ，也是力積電記憶體最大的代工客戶。主要代工夥伴力積電因先前將部
分廠房出售予 Micron(MU US) ，現有 12 吋晶圓廠中 P1 與 P2 廠原生產邏輯
代工，在 3Q26 調整至生產記憶體代工，但整體而言，記憶體代工產能釋出
受限，爭取晶圓代工與後段封測產能 (Wafer & Backend) 成為晶豪科當前最
關鍵的營運課題。  
力積電於 07/2026 全面啟動新一波代工報價調漲，其中針對 DRAM 投片價
格大幅調升約 45% 。考量自晶圓投入、前段製造至出貨之生產週期 (Cycle 
time) 約需 5~6 個月，晶豪科配合客戶訂單展開拉貨後，該批高價晶圓成本
將於 4Q26 正式反映於銷貨成本 (COGS) 之中。主要封測代工夥伴南茂 (8150 
TT) 亦自 07/2026 起調漲 20% 之封測費用，受限於約 6 個月之封測與成品週
轉週期，亦遞延至 11/2026 正式認列於營業成本。  
然而，由於市場端面臨華邦電等同業對成熟製程 DDR2 與 DDR3 減供或停
供的結構性缺口，終端訂單滿足率目前僅達 60% 至 70%，晶豪科具備極為強
勁的賣方定價話語權。在 4Q26 產品 ASP 持續走高，且漲幅 10~20% 的帶動
下 ， 晶豪科有能力將晶圓與封測端增加的成本全數轉嫁予客戶端 ， 支撐 4Q26
毛利率穩健維持在 3Q26 之 77.44% 歷史高檔區間。預估晶豪科 4Q26 營收
243.02 億元， QoQ+9.72% ，稅後淨利 117.49 億元， QoQ+9.20% ，稅後 EPS 
39.41 元。  
預估晶豪科 2027 年營收 898.37 億元， YoY+32.77% ，稅後獲利 380.72 億
元， YoY+24.91% ，稅後 EPS 127.72 元：  
晶豪科的產品組合，記憶體佔營收比重最高，約 95%。記憶體當中， DRAM
佔 65% 左右，以 KGD 為主。其他則為 NAND 、 NOR 以及其他
MCP/eMCP/eMMC 等。 DRAM 當中， DDR2 和 DDR3 佔目前 DRAM 出貨量
50% 。  
市場競爭格局方面， AI 伺服器對 HBM 多層晶圓堆疊的強勁需求大幅吸納
三大原廠先進 DRAM 產能，引發傳統記憶體供給端嚴重缺口，缺貨潮由
DDR5 與 DDR4 蔓延至 DDR3 、 DDR2 及 NOR/NAND Flash 。同業大廠如華
邦電基於晶圓廠經濟效益考量 ， 產能優先配置予大容量 DDR4 產品 ， 對DDR2
與 DDR3 採取停供或減供措施，其中華邦電之 DDR2 僅保留供應特定策略
夥伴，大幅限縮市場流通供給。目前晶豪科 DDR2 與 DDR3 合計營收比重
超過 50%，在同業減供退出的趨勢下，晶豪科已成為市場核心主要供應商，
市占地位結構性擴張。  
終端需求方面， AI PC、筆記型電腦與智慧型手機單機搭載記憶體容量提升，
加上實體 AI( 如人形機器人與自駕載具 )逐步落地，帶動非 HBM 記憶體需求
持穩增長。消費性電子端雖因記憶體 ASP 漲幅較大出現部分觀望情緒，惟
晶豪科高階與商務應用拉貨依然暢旺，客戶訂單滿足率受限於產能瓶頸，整

體營運動能無虞。展望 2027 年營運，管理層指出全球傳統記憶體產能供給
增長極為有限，供需失衡格局短期難以扭轉，預期市場供不應求狀態將延續
至 2027 年全年，支撐產品報價結構性維持堅挺， 2027 年整體營運維持樂觀
基調。預估晶豪科 2027 年營收 898.37 億元， YoY+32.77% ，稅後獲利 380.72

估未來現金股利發放率將落在 30% 至 50% 區間。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

00

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
여전히 강력하다 
3Q26 영업이익 83 조원으로 예상 상회 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 83조원 (+38% QoQ)으로 예상을 상회했다. 메모
리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340원에서 1,315원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026년 예상 FCF의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

2026-10-29

유틸리티업종

◼ 목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적, 소규모 SMR 수주 등은 긍정적 
◼ 구체적인 미국 원전 사업개발 시작, 2H27 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
◼ 2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조

목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적 양호, 소규모 SMR 수주는 긍정적 
투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 85,000원으로 하향. 이제는 성장 스토리보다
는 실질적인 원전 수주 확대가 필요한 상황. 의미 있는 수준의 원전 수주 확
대는 2H27부터 본격화될 가능성 존재. 다만, 단기적인 원전 수주 모멘텀 부
족 등을 감안해 6개월 목표주가를 현실화했음 
1) 3Q26 연결기준 영업이익 2,612억원(+91%, 이하 YoY), 에너빌리티 부문 
영업이익 1,211억원(+179%) 등 양호한 실적 기록. 이는 에너빌리티의 가스
터빈, 원전 기자재 매출 증가 등에 기인, 2) 10월 29일 NuScale과 1개 모듈 
주기기 공급 계약을 체결했으며, 4Q26 X-energy와 4개 모듈의 일부 장납기 
품목/소재 수주가 예상되는 등 2Q26 ~ 3Q26 TerraPower와의 일부 품목 계
약에 이어 개별 건의 규모는 작지만 다수의 SMR 관련 기자재 수주가 늘어나
는 점은 긍정적. 3) 최근 폴란드 AP1000 3기의 선발주 장납기 품목 확정(폴
란드 공급업체의 대상 생산 품목 확정, 원자로 압력용기 및 증기발생기는 미
확정)되었으며, 미국 원전 프로젝트가 구체화되기 시작. 4) 향후 AP1000향 
공급 기자재 품목 확대 가능성도 존재. 이는 2H27 본격적인 원전 수주금액 
확대로 이어질 것으로 기대

미국 원전은 구체적 사업개발 시작, 2H27 이후 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
2026년 6월 DOE의 ‘원전 공급망 금융지원 사업 발표(AP1000 10기)’, 8월 
TVA의 ‘2026 IRP’에 2040년까지 최대 원전 5.4GW 승인, 9월 한국과 미국 
상무부 간의 원전 투자 8기 MOU 체결 등을 통해 미국 원전 건설 재개 정책 
및 제도 개편이 구체화되고 강화되었음. 미국 원전 시장은 4Q26 ~ 2027년 
구체적인 사업개발 단계를 거쳐(부지 선정, 원전 운영 SPC 설립, 금융계약체
결, COL 신청 등), 2H27 ~ 2028년 장납기품목 선발주, 2028 ~ 2029년 건
설 계약 단계 진입 예상. 동사는 4Q26 미국 및 국내 가스터빈, 국내 공공 해
상풍력 프로젝트용 터빈, 폴란드 AP1000 주기기 등 미국 원전 이외 분야에
서의 수주 확대 예상. 현재 Rolls-Royce 등과도 SMR 주기기 공급 관련 논의 
중이며, NuScale SMR은 2027년 PPA 체결 시, COL 및 FID 이전 SMR 모듈 
11기(4Q26 1기 계약)의 수주가 가능할 것으로 기대

2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조 
대우건설·두산에너빌리티 컨소시엄의 체코 원전 시공 계약 협상은 종료되었
지만, 2027년 7월 신규 시공사와 계약 예정. 동사는 입찰에 다시 참여할 예
정. 이번 이슈의 핵심 쟁점은 이미 체코 측과 공사비총액(Lump-sum) 계약을 
맺은 한수원이 시공사와 총액계약을 맺기를 원한 것임. 이번 이슈로 체코 두
코바니 원전 EPC 프로젝트 수익성에 대한 우려가 발생할 수 있지만, 실제 건
설 과정에서 추가 비용이 공사비 계약금액(예비비 포함)을 초과할지는 예단하
기 어려움. 하도급 관리, 공기 준수 등 시공능력의 중요성이 강조되는 상황. 
동사는 3년 전부터 체코에 시공 인력 상주하며 시공 환경 자료 확보, 초기 인
허가, 설계 업무 관여해 왔으며, 주기기 공급사로서 기자재 특성을 활용해 설
치까지 수행 가능한 경쟁력 등을 바탕으로 성공적 수주를 목표하고 있음

표 2. 웨스팅하우스의 미국 및 글로벌 신규 원전 프로젝트 현황 (초기 사업발굴 단계 제외)  
 프로젝트·사업 기수 발전용량 Cameco 분류 단계 및 비고 
미국 DOE 공급망 금융지원 사업 최대 10기 최대 11.5GW 장납기 품목 조달 금융지원, 7개 유틸리티와 논의 중 
 미 상무부 전략적 협력사업 최대 10기 최대 11.5GW 정부 협력사업(한국은 6기 투자/건설) 
 V.C. Summer 2·3호기 공사 재개 2기 2.3GW 장납기 품목 발주·EPC(LLI/EPC) 
폴란드 Lubiatowo-Kopalino 3기 3.5GW 장납기 품목 발주·EPC(LLI/EPC)

표 3. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업

지원 대상 최대 5개 프로젝트, 프로젝트당 AP1000 2기, 합계 10기

조달 방식 Westinghouse가 참여 기업과 함께 장납기품목을 고정가격으로 조달

DAISHIN SECURITIES

표 4. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업의 대상 장납기품목(LLI; Long-Lead Items) 
 품목 설명  대출과 관련된 확인 근거 
원자로압력용기 
(RPV; Reactor Pressure Vessel) 
핵연료와 노심을 수용하고 원자로 냉각재의 압력을 유지하는 
용기. 앞서 설명한 격납용기와 구분되는 원자로 주기기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
증기발생기 
(SG; Steam Generator) 
원자로에서 나온 열을 전달받아 터빈을 돌릴 증기를 만드는 대
형 열교환기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
사전제작 구조 모듈 
(PSM; Prefabricated Structural Modules) 
원전 건물의 벽체·바닥 등 구조물을 모듈로 미리 제작하는 방
식의 구성품. AP1000에는 강판과 콘크리트로 구성하는 구조 
모듈이 사용됨 
DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23. 다만, 미국 대
출자료에 개별 모듈 코드까지 열거되지는 않음 
원자로냉각재펌프 
(RCP; Reactor Coolant Pump) 
원자로 냉각재를 순환시켜 원자로에서 발생한 열을 증기발생
기로 전달하는 펌프 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
격납용기 
(CV; Containment Vessel) 
원자로압력용기와 주요 설비를 둘러싸며 방사성 물질의 외부 
방출을 억제하는 대형 강철 밀폐 구조물 
DOE EDF 담당자의 직접 발언에서 확인 — 
2026.10.20 공개 인터뷰 
자료: DOE, 대신증권 Research Center

### Page 1

软银 2032 年到期美元债单⽇下跌 0.9 美分，亚洲投资级美元债利差升⾄ 2025 年 7 ⽉以来最
宽。不过，信贷市场跌幅仍相对克制，部分机构认为当前更像拥挤交易去化与估值重置，
AI ⻓期算⼒需求尚未动摇。
摘要：
⼈⼯智能股票的抛售浪潮已从股市蔓延⾄信贷市场，软银集团⾸当其冲，旗下美元债创下近⼗
周最⼤单⽇跌幅。
软银 2032 年到期美元债周五下跌 0.9 美分，其股价盘中⼀度重挫 16% 。与此同时，亚洲投资级美
元债利差扩⼤⾄少 3 个基点，交投活跃的亚洲⾼收益债也承压明显。信贷市场的波动叠加财政⾚
字攀升、油价⾛⾼及通胀升温等多重压⼒，令本已因 AI ⽀出热潮⽽获得提振的全球经济⾯临新的
下⾏⻛险。
此番抛售是全球信贷市场更⼤范围重新定价的延续。由 AI 热潮驱动的举债狂潮已推⾼收益率，⽽
本轮股市动荡进⼀步加速了这⼀进程。据彭博汇编数据，亚洲投资级美元债平均利差周四已升
⾄ 73 个基点，为 2025 年 7 ⽉以来最宽。
信贷抛售⼒度仍逊于股市
尽管信贷市场承压，但此轮抛售的烈度明显低于权益市场。 CreditSights 分析师 Zerlina Zeng 指

出，美国国债收益率下⾏为关注总体收益率的投资者提供了⼀定缓冲。
" 美国和亚洲市场的抛售整体较为克制， "Zeng 表⽰。她补充称， 10 ⽉份的⼤幅下跌已吸引不少
投资者逢低买⼊部分亚洲⾼收益债，其中包括表现尚可的房地产信⽤债。

China: Trade to remain strong as inﬂation
edges lower
China will release October inﬂation and trade data. We expect CPI

inﬂation to edge down to 0.8% YoY, from 0.9% in September,
suggesting that consumer price pressures remain subdued amid
continued weakness in domestic demand. By contrast, PPI inﬂation
is expected to rise further to 5.3% YoY, from 5.0%, driven mainly by

Export and import growth are expected to accelerate to 40.7% YoY

slightly to $131.2bn, from $133.5bn in September, as import
growth accelerates.
Taiwan: Technology demand supports strong
trade
Taiwan releases October trade data on Monday. We expect export
growth to moderate to 51.3% YoY, from 60.9% in September. Even
so, growth should remain exceptionally strong, supported by
demand for semiconductors and other AI-related products. Imports
are expected to accelerate to 69.2% YoY, from 51.7%, reﬂecting

strong demand for semiconductor equipment, components and
advanced materials. Despite faster import growth, the trade surplus
is forecast to widen to $25.6bn, from $23.6bn in September,
highlighting the continued strength of Taiwan’s technology export
cycle.

Document 310: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause , however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downsid e 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would  remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widel y 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is  
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals” . By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutini sed. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA 1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike.  But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, t he decision is justified by the need to further analy se risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that an y rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United St ates). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the H ICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1% -2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Anis Bensaidani   
Economist, United States, Japan 
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

Document 334: EcoNews of 26 October 2026 (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 26 October 2026 (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by a continued global selloff in tech
stocks, leading to safe haven flows into US Treasuries, Japanese Go -
vernment bonds and German bunds. As a result, their yields fell des -
pite energy prices surging to or past their Spring highs. Economic data
generally showed continued resilience. French bonds were under rising

pressure, but Moody's unexpected decision to maintain its France ra -
ting unchanged could, at the margin, help stabilise the spread. The Fed,
ECB and BOJ should be on hold next week while signaling further rate
action is likely.
EUROPEAN UNION
A win for securitization. The Council and the European Parliament have
reached a provisional political agreement on measures to revive and
simplify the EU’s securitization market. The updated framework will

reduce banks’ capital requirements for the safest and most well-struc-
tured transactions, notably for a newly introduced “resilient” category
of securitization. As a key component of the “One Europe, One Market”
initiative and the EU’s Savings and Investment Union agenda, this aims
to channel increased lending to households and businesses. The rele -
vant legal texts must now be finalised.
EUROZONE
Expansion continues. The flash PMIs weakened in October, mainly on

the back of a decline in France. The EZ composite index dipped to 51.9
(-1.2 m/m), dragged down by services (51.7, -1.3 m/m), although ma -
nufacturing also dipped (52.4, -0.5 m/m). Both remain in expansion
territory, and manufacturing new export orders rose to their highest
level since early 2022. Price signals were also rather reassuring, with
the services output price index easing to 54.9 (-1.7 m/m). Households
are more cautious: consumer confidence fell for a second consecutive

month to a five-month low (-17.9). Households’ inflation expectations
edged up at short horizons (+0.1 pp to 3.6% at one year and 3.1% at
three years), but long-term expectations remained anchored, with the
five-year median stable at 2.5%.
France
Rising market stress despite decent economic news; a reprieve from
Moody’s. The spread on French 10-year bond relative to Germany’s
peaked to 174 bps on Friday, the highest since 2011 and only 16 bps
short of its all-time high, as markets price in anticipation of worst-case

scenarios regarding the country’s ability to repair its public finances.
The 10Y yield briefly exceeded 5.1% before falling back to 5% (above
the US equivalent). Nonetheless, services output was stable in August,
following a 0.6% m/m increase in July, a much higher pace than in Q2.
The rebound is driven by the catering sector, the digital sector, business
services, and real estate. The INSEE business climate improved from
96 to 97 (long-term average = 100) in October, but the survey was

conducted before the riots. The main improvement was in manufactu-
ring, which rose from 101 to 105, driven by increased new orders in the
aeronautics sector, and to a lesser extent by the services sector (from
98 to 99). The PMI survey (conducted partially after the riots) was
less positive: the composite index fell to 48.4 (-2.7 m/m), with a sharp
decline in services (48.4; -2.8 m/m) and a slightly smaller decline in
manufacturing (49.3; -1.3 m/m). Moody's unexpectedly announced af -

ter the market close on Friday that it was maintaining France’s ra -
ting at Aa3 on account of France’s diversified economic structure and
sound public institutions, but maintained its negative outlook. This is
on par with the UK and one notch above S&P and Fitch. Moody’s made
clear keeping this rating is conditional on a budget being adopted that
reduces the fiscal deficit, along with an easing of social and market
stress going forward. Saturday’s demonstrations across the country

saw strong attendance but only limited violent incidents.
Germany
Prices continue to rise, but confidence surveys remain on an upward
trend. Producer prices rose 6.7% y/y in September (+2.1pp m/m; the
highest level in 3.5 years) due to higher prices for energy and inter -
mediate goods, primarily metals. The ZEW Economic Sentiment Index
improved beyond consensus forecasts in October and returned to its
highest level since May 2023 (-35.9; +11.2 m/m), albeit the Economic

Expectations Index fell to its lowest level since July (28.1; -6.6 m/m).
The recovery in economic activity continues, although a slowdown is
evident: the flash Composite PMI slowed (52.8; -1 m/m; the lowest in
2 months) due to weaker growth in the services sector (51.7; -1.1 m/m;
the lowest in 2 months) and in manufacturing output (55.1; -0.9 m/m;
the lowest in 3 months). Nevertheless, in both sectors, hiring is rising
slightly, supported by growth in export sales and the spillover effects of

investment plans. Despite persistently high input prices, selling prices
are slowing (reaching their lowest level since February in the manu -
facturing sector).
UNITED KINGDOM
Inflation, wage growth and activity accelerate. CPI inflation overshot
consensus on energy prices, printing at 0.4% m/m and 3.5% y/y in Sep-
tember (consensus: 3.4%, prior: 3.1%), its highest in 11 months. Core
CPI came in at 2.8% y/y (up 0.2 pp), in line with consensus. Pressures

remained persistent according to the PPI at 8.3% y/y (prior: 6.7%). La -
bour market and activity data also surprised positively. Payroll growth
reached 102k in August (3m/3m), bringing the unemployment rate to
4.7% (down 0.2 pp), its lowest since July 2025. October PMI surveys
beat expectations in both manufacturing (52.8, consensus: 51.8, prior:
51.9) and services (53.1, consensus: 51.8, prior: 52.1). The only negative
news came from headline retail sales, falling 0.4% m/m in September,

with the core gauge (excluding petrol) deteriorating by -0.7% m/m.
UNITED STATES
Construction struggling, GENIUS Act implemented. Housing starts
dropped -5.2% m/m in September (-7.4% y/y). This third consecutive
monthly decline dragged the Q3 average to its lowest since Q2 2020.
Building permits also came in negative, at -0.2% m/m (-3.0% y/y), while
the NAHB homebuilder sentiment index plunged to its weakest since
2012. The average 30-year fixed mortgage rate eased to 7.32% (still

134bp above pre-war level), as the ongoing AI selloff pushed down
US treasury yields. The GENIUS Act final implementation rule from the
Office of the Comptroller of the Currency’s (OCC) leaves the door open
for stablecoin customers to receive yield or rewards. Issuers remain
prohibited from paying holders any form of interest or yield solely
in connection with the holding, use, or retention of payment stable -
coins. This represents a shift from the OCC’s February proposal and

has renewed concerns within the banking industry about deposit flight.
The rule will take effect on January 18, 2027.
JAPAN
Activity and inflation keep rising. Composite PMI was unchanged in
September at 52.3, solidly in expansion territory, as an improvement
in services activity offset slower output growth in manufacturing. The
Manufacturing PMI disappointed at 53.3 (-0.8 m/m; an 8-month low).
Underlying price pressures, meanwhile, extended. Headline CPI was

flat on a monthly basis, bringing the annual rate up to 2.1% (up 0.4 pp).
Core CPI (excl. fresh food) matched it at 2.1% y/y (up 0.4 pp), in line
with consensus. More importantly, the New Core index, which strips

EMERGING ECONOMIES

ASIA
China
New monetary policy tool and export controls. The central bank’s Go-
vernor Pan Gongsheng unveiled a plan to switch (“when appropriate”)
its key policy rate from the 7-day reverse repo rate (at 1.4% since May
2025) to the overnight reverse repo rate, in order to reflect develop -
ments in the interbank market. New export controls: The government
plans to add 40 key technologies to the list of technologies prohibited
or restricted for export, including in the AI (for ex. LLMs, computing

clusters), biotechnology and photovoltaic sectors.
Indonesia
BI kept policy rate unchanged at 5.75%, raised its deposit facility rate
by 25bp to 5% and announced a phased cut in reserve requirements
from 9% to 7% by December. This combination of limited FX support
and measures to support credit growth sends mixed signals amidst
pressures on the rupiah following MSCI’s downgrade warning and un-
dermines the central bank’s credibility. The rupiah has lost 1% against

the USD since mid-October, with little contagion effect on other Asian
currencies.
South Korea
The Bank of Korea (BoK) kept its policy rate unchanged at 3.0%, but
the tightening cycle may not be over. CPI inflation was 2.9% y/y in Sep-
tember, above the central bank’s target of 2%, while high oil prices,
rapid growth in Seoul housing prices and wage pressures (though
concentrated in the electronics sector) raise the risk of lasting infla -
tion pressure. As economic activity remains robust, supported by strong

exports and recovering investment, the BoK has scope to raise rates
further.
EMEA
Hungary
Monetary status quo and 2027 budget in consolidation mode. The
Central Bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 5.5%, as expected. A cau-
tious stance will likely prevail in the short term. Inflation has been
picking up in recent months, albeit mildly, and will continue to move
upward as the government recently removed the margin cap on fuel.
Separately, the Budget for 2027 and the medium-term fiscal plan show

Hungary’s goal to join the Eurozone; the government expects to reduce
the fiscal deficit to 4.9% of GDP in 2027 (from 7% expected in 2026) and
towards 2.8% of GDP by 2030.
Türkiye
Cautious monetary loosening. The Central Bank lowered its policy rate
by 100 bp to 36% at its October Monetary Policy Committee meeting,
in line with market expectations. The overnight lending and borrowing
rates were also reduced by 100 bp, to 39% and 34.5%, respectively.

The improvement in underlying inflation has yet to translate into a
sustained period of low inflation, justifying the CBRT’s preference for a
measured pace of monetary easing.
LATIN AMERICA
Brazil
President Lula da Silva was re-elected on Sunday for a fourth term
(with 51.8% of the vote), defeating far right-wing candidate Flavio Bol-
sonaro (48.2%). Lula will enter this new term facing significant econo -
mic and political challenges. Public debt has risen to more than 82% of

GDP , while high borrowing costs and rigid mandatory spending leave
limited room to reconcile fiscal consolidation with social spending and
investment. Restoring confidence in the fiscal outlook will be a key
challenge in the short term.
COMMODITIES
Pressure rising. Amid growing rumours of imminent re-escalation of
military operations in the Gulf, energy prices rose sharply this week,
with brent touching $117/b – near its April peak, and European gas

prices (TTF) rising to 97 €/MWh on Monday, their highest level since
Dec 2022. The Asian gas benchmark (JKM) is following the same trend
(4.6% w/w to 31.4 $/mbtu, equivalent to around 96 €/MWh). Diesel
crack spreads are slightly decreasing in Europe (-2.8% w/w on Monday)
and in the US (-4% w/w on Monday). The first Russian diesel tanker
intended to the US market left Russia this Friday and should supply the
US market in 2 weeks. US diesel average retail prices remain oriented

to the upside (0.7% w/w at 6.32 $/gallon on Monday). US commercial
crude inventories fell by 3.1 mb in the week ending 16 October, signi -
ficantly exceeding market expectations for a 340,000-barrel decline.

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Key points – September quarter 2026
• The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in labour market conditions. The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an
almost five-year run of increases. The unemployment rate is now at its highest level since September 2015.
• There were mixed messages in terms of jobs growth: the Household Labour Force Survey reported a 0.1% fall in employment, while the employer-

focused Quarterly Employment Survey suggested a modest rise. The Monthly Employment Indicator had also pointed to a small rise in jobs.
• The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in the participation rate. These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly large
increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error in the survey rather than a
genuine surge.
• We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message here is one of softness. Our

best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population.
• While the economy has returned to growth over the last year or so, hiring activity remains weak. This may be an ongoing legacy of employment trends in
previous years, when firms were relatively reluctant to lay people off during the slowdown, which then potentially left them overstaffed even as activity
started to pick up again. It’s not clear how long this effect can persist, though.

• Wage growth remains muted. Although cost-of-living pressures have increased on workers, the existing degree of spare capacity in the labour market
doesn’t give them much leverage to negotiate larger pay increases.
• Overall, we judge these results to be weaker than the RBNZ expected, in particular the rise in the unemployment rate. This data supports the views of
those on the policy committee who were less convinced in October about the potential for second-round inflation pressures in the economy.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
Employment
• The number of people employed fell by 0.1% in the September quarter, according to the
Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS). Previous quarters were revised down a little,
reflecting downward revisions to the working-age population estimates. Annual employment
growth slowed from 0.9% to 0.7%.
• This was substantially softer than our forecast of a 0.5% rise for the quarter. Our forecast
was based on the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI), a comprehensive measure that is

drawn from income tax data. While there are some differences in coverage for these two
measures, any significant divergence between the two is most likely to reflect sampling error
in the HLFS.
• Meanwhile, the employer-focused Quarterly Employment Survey (QES) recorded a 0.1% rise
in the number of filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. (This survey is of course vulnerable
to sampling error as well.)
• We’re most inclined towards the MEI measure of employment, given the breadth of its

coverage. But our best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not
yet fast enough to absorb the growth in the adult population – hence the further rise in the
unemployment rate.

### Page 4

Westpac New Zealand Economics
4
Labour force participation
• The surprising fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a drop in the participation rate
from 70.6% to 70.4%.
• These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were suggestive of sampling error in the
survey. Any such errors largely wash out when it comes to the unemployment rate, however,
which is why we recommend focusing on this measure.

• Setting aside the usual quarterly volatility, labour force participation remains caught
between two competing forces. One is cyclical weakness, especially among younger people,
who are finding it difficult to break into a “low-fire, low-hire” labour market. The teen
unemployment rate of around 25% is a record high going back to the start of the survey
in 1986.
• The other force is rising participation among the over-60s (who also make up a growing

share of the population). Kiwis have had a growing propensity to work beyond the NZ Super
age, as there is no penalty for doing so. It’s unclear to what extent this can be considered
an increase in the pool of available workers: older workers tend to have low mobility
between jobs, and if they find themselves out of work are more likely to leave the labour
force altogether.

### Page 5

Westpac New Zealand Economics
5
Unemployment and underutilisation
• The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7% in the September quarter. This was the
highest rate since September 2015.
• While there are other indicators of spare capacity in the labour market, we’re not convinced
that there is additional information to draw from them this time.
• The broader underutilisation measure, which includes discouraged jobseekers and part-
time workers wanting more hours, was steady at 13.8% after having risen sharply from

12.9% last quarter. This measure is both higher (by definition) and more variable than the
unemployment rate, but tells a similar story in that it’s around an 11-year high.
• There was a marked 1.6% fall in the number of hours worked (although ‘usual’ hours were
down just 0.4%). However, we suspect this was largely driven by Matariki – a floating holiday
that won’t be captured in the seasonal adjustment.
• This may also account for the 2.5% rise in part-time employment, against a 0.7% fall in

full-time employment. Some workers may have been pushed into the part-time category
(less than 30 hours a week) simply by virtue of only working four days during the
survey week.

### Page 6

Westpac New Zealand Economics
6
Hiring
• Business confidence surveys have shown a lift in hiring intentions in recent months, as firms
have shaken off the initial shock of the Iran conflict and the surge in fuel prices.
• However, those stated intentions aren’t obviously turning into reality yet. Job advertisements
were down about 1% for the September quarter in seasonally adjusted terms, and they
remain well below pre-Covid levels. Job ads will capture ‘churn’ within existing roles as well

as new job creation, but both of these would be associated with a strong labour market.
• With the working-age population growing at the same time, the result was a modest fall in
the vacancy rate, one of the RBNZ’s highest-ranked measures of labour market tightness.
• We estimate that there are around 1.9 people unemployed for every job vacancy, up from
around 1.8 people this time last year.
• Firms have told us that they were reluctant to lay off workers during the economic downturn

in 2023-24, as they were wary of the extreme labour shortages that had developed in the
previous boom years. As a result, they may find themselves still overstaffed even as demand
has started to pick up again, and haven’t needed to return to hiring yet.

### Page 7

Westpac New Zealand Economics
7
Population and migration
• The working-age population grew by around 1.0% over the last year, compared to a peak of
3.3% growth over 2023. Jobs growth (based on the less volatile MEI measure) is still lagging
behind population growth on an annual basis.
• The pool of potential workers is generally becoming older, with the Baby Boomers
increasingly reaching pension age and beyond, and with migrant arrivals tending to be an

older cohort than we saw pre-Covid – more in their 30s and 40s than their 20s.
• Net migration flows have started to pick up from their lows since late 2025. Departures of
New Zealanders have come down from their highs, though they remain above pre-Covid
levels, as the relative appeal of the Australian jobs market has narrowed.
• Foreign arrivals have picked up to some degree, although the growth appears to have been
mostly driven by students rather than those on work visas.

### Page 8

Westpac New Zealand Economics
8
Sectoral and regional trends
• Jobs growth has been relatively strong in government-connected sectors such as healthcare
and education, as well as in local and central government administration.
• Mining and utilities have seen strong growth in percentage terms, but neither of these
sectors are big employers in absolute terms.
• The construction sector is now starting to see a lift in employment, after a steep decline

over 2024-25. Residential building consents have risen strongly over the last year or so,
pointing to a growing pipeline of work for the homebuilding industry.
• Sectors that are connected to international tourism, such as transport and recreational
services, had previously seen strong employment growth, but this appears to have flattened
out in recent months.
• Employment trends have generally been more positive in the South Island than the North,

which likely reflects the greater influence of the agricultural sector – while not a large
employer in itself, farmers have benefited from high export prices and their spending has
flowed into other sectors.

### Page 9

Westpac New Zealand Economics
9
Wages
• The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter. Annual growth
remained around the lowest since 2021.
• While the overall result was in line with our forecast, the details were a little weaker than
we expected, with less contribution from private sector wages (up 0.5%) and a surprisingly
large 0.7% increase in the public sector.
• The distribution of pay rates has returned to pre-Covid trends. Only 44% of roles saw a

pay rise over the last year; of those roles that did see an increase, the average size has
converged on the 2-3% range.
• The LCI is not a measure of what workers actually receive – it reflects the cost to businesses
for a given unit of labour – and hence shouldn’t be compared to the inflation rate. A more
suitable measure is the analytical unadjusted LCI, which includes pay rises that are related
to productivity gains. This measure slowed from 3.1% to 2.9% on an annual basis, the lowest

since March 2021.
• There was a marked pickup in the proportion of firms reporting cost-of-living adjustments as
the main reason for pay increases. However, the size of those increases varied greatly, with
many of them below the rate of CPI inflation.
• This seems to reflect a shift in the justification for pay rises, rather than an overall increase in
wage pressures – firms are more likely to cite cost-of-living adjustments because they’re less
likely to cite demand-side pressures.

### Page 10

Westpac New Zealand Economics
10
International comparisons
• New Zealand’s unemployment rate is the 18th lowest in the OECD (out of 38 countries). This
ranking has been broadly unchanged over the last year or so.
• Notably, New Zealand’s unemployment rate is substantially above Australia’s, whereas
historically it has been lower on average. This shift in relative job opportunities has been a
key attractor for New Zealanders over the last couple of years – as it was in the early 2010s

when the Australian economy was benefiting from a boom in mining construction.
• However, that gap between unemployment rates is no longer widening, with the Australian
jobs market starting to soften this year as the RBA has raised interest rates to rein in inflation
(which was already running too high before the Iran conflict and the surge in oil prices). We
expect Australia’s unemployment to rise further over the year ahead.
• New Zealand has the 7th highest employment rate in the OECD (down from 6th last quarter).

We tend to have high rates of labour force participation for 15-64 year olds, and particularly
high participation for the over-65s, as the lack of means testing for NZ Superannuation
means there is no financial disincentive to working beyond pension age.

exports and investment and an intact semiconductor 
competitive moat. 
• AI is generating broader domestic spillovers, with

consumption.

energy imports and absorbing much of the increase in 
upstream costs.

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook

13 October 2026

Ma Tieying, CFA

### Page 2

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook October 13, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

Global backdrop

The global landscape remains complex, 
shaped by the interplay of AI, energy 
prices and tariffs, with significant 
implications for growth, inflation and 
financial conditions.

1) AI

The debate over the durability of the AI 
investment boom continues.  Tailwinds 
include technological advances, broader 
adoption, and s trategic and geopolitical 
considerations. Headwinds range from 
uncertainty over investment returns  and 
hyperscaler financing pressure to 
physical bottlenecks , safety concerns, 
and regulatory uncertainty.

Hyperscaler financing pressure has 
attracted increasing attention . In the 
early stages of the AI investment boom, 
hyperscaler capex was largely financed 
by internal cash flow. This is now 
changing, as free cash flow is expected 
to turn negative from 4Q, requiring major 
players to rely on debt issuance and 
other external financing increasingly.

At the same time, higher global interest 
rates are raising the cost of AI 
investment. The Fed resumed rate hikes 
at its September meeting, while global 
bond yields remain under upward 
pressure amid persistent inflation and 
elevated public and private borro wing 
needs. Our baseline assumes US rates 
will rise by 50bps this year and a further 
25bps in 2027 to 4.50%, with global 
long-term rates also remaining under 
pressure. This could increasingly weigh 
on the economics and pace of AI 
investment.

US-China AI competition is also drawing 
increasing attention.  Across the five 
layers of the AI ecosystem, China is 
making rapid progress in energy, 
infrastructure, AI models and 
applications, supported by its large scale, 
cost-effective domestic manufacturing 
supply chains and huge consumer 
market, which provides a wide range of 
real-world scenarios for AI adoption. The 
emergence of a more integrated and 
cost-competitive Chinese AI ecosystem 
could increasingly challe nge the US 
ecosystem, which currently operates at a 
relatively higher cost.

China, however, remains relatively weak 
in semiconductors.  Around half of 
China's domestic AI chip market still 
relies on foreign suppliers, including 
lower-end NVIDIA chips. This 
semiconductor bottleneck remains a key 
constraint on China's AI development. In 
our view, Taiwanese semiconductor 
companies' competiti ve moat therefore 
remains largely intact in the near term.

to 39% in 2027. Similarly, global 
semiconductor sales remained strong 
through 3Q, while WSTS expects growth 
to moderate from 90% this year to 27 % 
in 2027.

2) Energy

The US -Iran conflict remains at a 
standoff, keeping the energy -price 
outlook highly uncertain.  New 
developments are likely after the US 
midterm elections in November, with 
both further escalation and de -
escalation possible. Crude oil prices have 
remained elevated at around 
USD100/bbl amid a persistent 
geopolitical risk premium . In contrast, 
refined product prices have risen more 
sharply as refining disruptions and 
tighter product logistics have amplified 
the supply shock. Natural gas prices 
have also increased sharply, reflecting 
disruptions to LNG supply and limited 
alternative shipping routes.

Taiwan has been actively diversifying 
its energy import sources to cushion the 
impact. LNG imports from Qatar have 
fallen to zero since April, while imports 
from the US and other suppliers have 
surged to offset the shortfall, helping 
maintain stable domestic electricity 
supply.

                                                                                                                                            5

3) Tariffs

US tariff actions are likely to accelerate 
after the US midterm elections in 
November. Two major tariff measures 
remain in the pipeline. The Section 301 
forced-labor tariff was implemented in 
July, while final tariff action under the 
Section 301 overcapacity investigation, 
which covers 16 economies, remains 
pending. Meanwhile, Phase 1 of the 
Section 232 semiconductor tariffs was 
implemented in January, while Phase 2, 
covering a broader range of 
semiconductor-related products, 
remains pending.

Taiwan has secured a relatively 
favourable framework through its trade 
and investment agreements with the 
US. Taiwan signed an Agreement on 
Reciprocal Trade (ART) and an 
Investment MOU with the US earlier this 
year. The ART establishes a 15% US tariff 
rate on Taiwanese goods, while the MOU 
provides Taiwan with preferential 
treatment under Section 232, including 
for semiconductors. The ART has yet to 
enter into force, and it remains to be 
seen how the final Section 301 tariff 
burden will interact with the 15% ART 
framework. Meanwhile, major Taiwanese 
companies such as TSMC are expanding 
their US investments under the 
Investment MOU, which could support 
relatively favourable treatment for 
Taiwan when broader Section 232 
semiconductor tariffs are introduced.

Taiwan 4Q outlook

Taiwan has navigated the complex 
global environment relatively well so far 
this year, supported by robust AI exports 
and investment, diversified energy 
imports, and efforts to absorb higher 
upstream energy costs. Some 
normalisation is likely in 4Q26 –2027, as 
the AI -driven growth boost moderates 
and domestic energy prices face greater 
pressure to adjust.

1) Growth

GDP growth remains broadly on track 
with our expectations. High -frequency 
indicators suggest that GDP growth 
should remain around 12% yoy in 3Q, 
broadly in line with 12.9% in 2Q but below 
the 15.4% peak in 1Q. Growth is expected 
to moderate to a high -single-digit pace 
from 4Q onwards. We maintain our full -
year GDP growth forecasts at 11.6% for 
2026 and 5.6% for 2027.

The AI cycle remains the key driver of 
Taiwan's growth.  Information and 
communication products and electronic 
components accounted for nearly 80% 
of total exports in the first nine months 
of this year, while manufacturing 
accounts for nearly 50% of gross fixed

capital formation. From 4Q onwards, 
export growth is expected to moderate 
from exceptionally strong levels to a 
more normal but still robust pace, 
broadly following the trajectory of 
global hyperscaler capex and 
semiconductor sales. Investment is likely 
to follow a similar path.

For now, Taiwanese technology 
companies continue to report robust 
revenue growth, led by semiconductors, 
ODMs/EMS, and PCs and servers. 
Capacity utilisation at leading Taiwanese 
foundries has also continued to rise, 
suggesting that investment demand 
remains strong.

The AI boom is also beginning to 
generate broader spillovers to the 
domestic economy. The AI-driven equity 
rally has generated significant wealth 
effects, with the TAIEX up 70% year-to-
date and Taiwan becoming the world's 
fifth-largest stock market. Taiwanese 
households have benefited from the 
appreciation of financial assets, with 
equities and mutual funds accounting for 
nearly 30% of total household financial 
assets. The equity rally has also 
generated positive income effects. Wage 
growth has picked up, led by the services 
sector, particularly financial services, as 
strong earnings at financial 
institutions—especially securities 
firms—have prompted higher 
compensation to retain talent. These 
wealth and income gains should support 
household consumption, creating a 
positive feedback loop that broadens the 
benefits of the AI boom across the wider 
economy.
